**Summary:**
- `mean_f1_fixed_05` = 0.7940 (fixed θ=0.5)
- `mean_f1_adaptive_mlp` = 0.7906 (MLP predicted θ)
- `mean_f1_oracle` = 0.7953 (best θ per config)
- `delta_mlp_minus_fixed` = **-0.0034** (MLP is WORSE than fixed!)


- `gap_closed_pct` = -40219696% (overflow/division by zero issue — means adaptive closes a negative gap)
- `theta_exact_match` = 1/18 (only 1 config matched oracle θ exactly)
- `theta_within_005` = 5/18
- `theta_within_010` = 8/18
- `win_tie_loss_vs_fixed` = 5-0-13 (MLP loses on 13/18 configs!)

The MLP is heavily biased toward predicting low thresholds around 0.3 or 0.35, which explains why it underperforms the fixed 0.5 baseline on most configurations.

The root cause is that the MLP outputs negative values for many datasets (like -0.82 for CoNLL) which get clamped to the minimum of 0.3, revealing a calibration problem. On high-degeneracy datasets like CoNLL where the oracle uses much higher thresholds (0.4-0.65), this systematic underprediction causes consistent losses, while on lower-degeneracy datasets like FewNERD where oracle thresholds cluster around 0.3-0.4, the MLP's bias occasionally aligns with the target.

The fundamental issue is that leave-one-config-out CV with only 18 configurations provides far too little training data for an MLP with 7 input features to learn a reliable mapping—the model essentially memorizes or defaults to a conservative threshold. The negative result (-0.34pp compared to fixed θ=0.5) is statistically unsurprising given this severe data scarcity. More importantly, the oracle-fixed gap is only 0.13pp, meaning there's minimal room for improvement over the fixed threshold anyway, which suggests θ=0.5 is already quite robust and adaptive thresholding offers limited practical benefit.

The registry entry still shows RUNNING despite the experiment having completed output—a stale entry that wasn't updated. I'm also noticing a discrepancy: the results contain 18 configs but the registry spec lists 16, suggesting 2 additional configs were added after the initial specification. Looking at the per-config breakdown, I see 18 total across LLaMA and Qwen models with three datasets and three seeds each.

Now I'm spotting something odd with the Qwen_FewNERD_s42 config—it has only 4351 instances while all other FewNERD configs have 32565. That's suspiciously close to the subset size used in another experiment, which could indicate data contamination or a wrong file reference. The key findings are that the MLP underperforms the fixed threshold by 0.34 percentage points and only wins on 5 out of 18 configs, the oracle gap is tiny at just 0.13 percentage points above the fixed threshold, and 18 configs is likely too small for the MLP to learn anything meaningful—a methodological limitation.

I'm also noticing the MLP's raw theta predictions are often negative, suggesting it's not properly calibrated and getting clamped to the minimum. The registry still shows RUNNING status, which might mean the parallel workers mentioned in the spec are still processing other variants, though I've only found the v2_mlp_results.json file so far.

  "text": "## 审查报告 — exp_e_theta_adaptive\n\n**结论**: WARNING\n\n### 发现\n\n- [severity: warning] **Registry 状态过期**：实验 RUNNING 已超 11 小时（updated_at=15:39 UTC），本地无对应进程。Spec 声明 \"5 parallel workers with different feature/model variants\"，但 artifacts 中仅存 v2_mlp_results.json（1/5 变体）。其余 4 个变体（LogReg + 其他特征子集）无输出。可能 crashed 或未启动。建议标记为 DONE (partial) 并补跑缺失变体。\n\n- [severity: warning] **MLP 变体结果为 negative**：\n  - 固定 θ=0.5 mean F1 = 0.7940\n  - MLP adaptive θ mean F1 = 0.7906（**-0.34pp**，更差）\n  - Oracle θ mean F1 = 0.7953（仅 +0.13pp headroom）\n  - Win/Tie/Loss vs 固定: 5/0/13\n  - MLP 在 13/18 configs 上输给固定 θ=0.5\n  - `pred_theta_raw` 频繁为负值（如 CoNLL 配置 -0.82），clamped 到最低 0.3\n\n  **诊断**：n=18 configs 做 leave-one-config-out CV 训练 MLP（7 features, ~64 parameters）严重过拟合/欠拟合。17 个训练点不够学习映射。但更重要的发现是 **oracle-fixed gap 仅 0.13pp** —— 即使知道最优 θ，提升也极有限。**θ=0.5 是一个强 default，adaptive θ 不是论文的有效贡献方向**。\n\n- [severity: warning] **数据不一致**：Qwen_FewNERD_s42 显示 n_instances=4351，其余 FewNERD 配置均为 32565。4351 与 exp_b2_entity_verifier 的子集大小相同。可能使用了错误的数据文件，导致该配置的结果不可比。\n\n- [severity: info] **Oracle θ 分布**：CoNLL 偏好高 θ（0.4-0.65），FewNERD 偏好低 θ（0.3-0.4），SciERC 偏好低 θ（0.3-0.4）。高 degeneracy 数据集需更严格过滤阈值——但差异极小（<0.2pp within θ=0.4-0.5 range），不足以支撑 adaptive θ 的 narrative。\n\n### 建议\n- 将 exp_e_theta_adaptive 标记为 DONE (negative) —— adaptive θ headroom 不足，不应进入论文主贡献\n- 修复 Qwen_FewNERD_s42 数据不一致后再引用具体数字\n- 可在 appendix 中简要提及\"θ=0.5 是鲁棒 default\"作为 engineering finding",

Title: \\\"Strong Predictors, Weak Selectors: Addressing the Correlation-Selection Gap in Self-Consistency for Named Entity Recognition\\\". **Round 10 critical fixes**: (1) B2 in-domain +3.62pp CONFIRMED LEAKED (train-on-test) — deleted from paper, awaiting out-of-fold re-evaluation; cross-dataset negative transfer valid; (2) P \\\"Impossibility Theorem\\\" downgraded to \\\"Degeneracy Decomposition\\\" — Theorem 1 trivial, Theorem 2 tautological, \\\"information-theoretic\\\" overclaim removed; (3) Ceiling-gap framing retained; (4) MBR v3 retained; (5) FewNERD-primary retained; (6) N Inverse Scaling core finding solid (θ=0.5 immune). Target: Main 4.3+. Scale: 7B→14B→32B→72B Qwen2.5 + 8B cross-family (Qwen3+LLaMA). 14B/32B training in progress.\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 **Main Conference** (via ARR May 2026)\\n- Deadline: ARR May cycle, 2026-05-25\\n\\n## Core Claims (D157 updated — TRIPLE CONTRIBUTION: QE + diagnostic + prescriptive)\\n\\n### Claim 1 (PRIMARY — Dual Contribution: QE Framework + Selection Diagnostic)\\n**Positive contribution (A)**: Confidence signals reliably predict structured IE output quality at the population level (AUROC up to 0.82, ρ up to 0.45), enabling practical QE/selective prediction.\\n**Diagnostic contribution (B)** (D154 revised): Selection viability depends on LP signal quality and dataset characteristics, not degeneracy alone:\\n- **Degeneracy as headroom predictor**: High degeneracy eliminates selection headroom (CoNLL 47.4% degen → 0 net gain). Within fixed dataset-model settings, degeneracy causally modulates LP effectiveness (SciERC epoch ablation: −1.18/+0.74/+0.99pp at 3/5/10 epochs; per-entity-type within Few-NERD ρ=−0.881). But across datasets, degeneracy alone does NOT predict LP selection direction (23-point cross-configuration ρ=0.052, p=0.81).\\n- **LP-quality alignment as primary predictor**: LP must track entity-level F1 within instances. Few-NERD within-instance ρ(LP,F1) ≈ 0.5-0.66 → LP selection +1.36pp (p<0.05). SciERC ρ(LP,F1) ≈ 0.18-0.24 → LP fails despite similar degeneracy (17.8% vs 13.8%). This is the primary determinant of LP selection success across datasets.\\n- **Structural signals (SJ/FK/EM/VC)**: universally fail regardless — instance-level aggregates cannot rank candidates by construction.\\n- **Multi-causal diagnosis (D154)**: (1) degeneracy modulates headroom within settings; (2) LP-quality alignment determines cross-dataset selection success; (3) LoRA rank=8 reduces degeneracy (7.2% vs 17.8%) AND improves LP spread (0.057 vs 0.041 nats) but SciERC's weak LP-F1 alignment (ρ~0.24) still prevents selection success → LP alignment is the binding constraint, degeneracy is a within-setting modulator.\\n\\nThe framing is NOT \\\"selection fails\\\" (pure negative) but \\\"we characterize WHEN LP works (strong LP-F1 alignment), WHY it fails elsewhere (weak alignment + compression), and WHAT distinguishes LP from structural signals\\\" (empirical characterization with diagnostic implications).\\n\\n**Three-layer argument + Capability-diversity tension**:\\n- Layer A (design property, noted not discovered): 4/5 signals are instance-level aggregates → cannot rank candidates by construction. This is a conceptual clarification — the \\\"methodological premise\\\" — not an empirical finding.\\n- Layer B (empirical — instance degeneracy): 21-58% of instances produce identical F1 across all N samples, eliminating selection headroom. Within-instance ρ=0.524 (SciERC) and 0.994 (CoNLL) — signals ARE correlated within instances, but degeneracy makes this irrelevant. Non-degenerate instances show MBR closing 23-41% of oracle gap. Selection recovers T=1.0 sampling degradation (LP 0.647 vs Random 0.610, +3.7pp) but only matches T=0 greedy (0.645 macro) at 8× inference cost — zero marginal gain.\\n- Layer C (empirical — LP-quality alignment, revised D123): LP selection requires adequate within-instance correlation with task quality. Few-NERD ρ(LP,F1)≈0.5-0.66 → selection succeeds (+1.36pp). SciERC ρ(LP,F1)≈0.18-0.24 → selection fails even when LP range is adequate (rank=8: 0.057 nats). LP compression (median range 0.041 nats on SciERC full-rank) is a contributing factor but NOT the sole explanation — rank=8 expands LP range to 0.057 yet still fails. Entity-token LP shows 4× variance but WORSE selection (-2.0pp). Free-form ablation confirms compression is model/task intrinsic for NER (std ratio 0.97).\\n- **Capability-diversity tension** (unifying insight, D111): Model capability → output convergence → instance degeneracy → vanishing selection headroom. Evidence: CoNLL (91% F1) → 58% degeneracy, SciERC (66% F1) → 21%, WNUT (48% F1) → ~0%. Scale dimension: 8B → 21% degen vs 4B → 10.4% on SciERC. This is NOT an \\\"artifact\\\" — it is a **consequence of capability**, a structural inevitability. The better the model, the less test-time scaling can help via unsupervised selection.\\n\\n**Distinction from MBR literature**: Eikema et al.'s MBR failure = utility function misalignment. Our failure = utility IS aligned (within-instance ρ=0.524) but headroom eliminated by degeneracy — qualitatively novel mechanism.\\n\\n**Key evidence (score compression)**:\\n- Non-tied subset (40%): logprob selection +2.9pp vs greedy (p=0.01), within-instance ρ: 0.08→0.39\\n- Full set: logprob +0.6pp, bootstrap p=0.39, Cohen's d=0.038 (negligible), 95% CI [-3.0, +4.2]pp\\n- Oracle headroom: NER +13pp, RE +18pp → information exists but signals can't access it\\n\\n**Key evidence (free-form ablation)** [DONE — exp_freeform_ablation, NEGATIVE]:\\n- LP distribution: constrained std=0.0206, free-form std=0.0199 (ratio 0.97, virtually unchanged)\\n- Tied fraction: 59.7% → 57.7% (-2.0pp, negligible)\\n- NER selection: +0.59pp → +0.18pp (both negligible vs greedy)\\n- RE selection: -0.68pp → +1.69pp (flipped positive — secondary finding)\\n- NER VC rho: 0.379 → 0.409 (slight QE improvement, selection still fails)\\n- Parse rate: 99.95% (fine-tuned model retains JSON format without grammar)\\n- **Conclusion**: Score compression is model/task intrinsic. The gap is universal.\\n\\n**Key evidence (scale ablation)** [DONE — 2×2 matrix: {CoNLL, SciERC} × {Qwen3-8B, Qwen3-4B}]:\\n- CoNLL NER: 4B greedy_f1=0.9234 (>8B 0.9123, +1.11pp), SJ_ρ=0.4217 (≈8B 0.4357, −1.4pp), FK_ρ=0.4181 (>8B 0.3998, +1.83pp)\\n- SciERC NER: 4B SJ_ρ=0.4221 (>8B 0.3825, +3.96pp), FK_ρ=0.3258 (>8B 0.2590, +6.68pp)\\n- SciERC RE: 4B SJ_ρ=0.2673 (>8B 0.2441, +2.32pp), FK_ρ=0.3593 (>8B 0.2782, +8.11pp); struct>surface ✗ at both scales\\n- **Conclusion**: Correlation strength maintained or improved at 4B across all 4 cells. The gap originates from within-instance signal misalignment, not model capacity. Task difficulty (SciERC RE zero-F1 rate) remains the primary driver, consistent across scales.\\n- CoNLL 4B headroom: 1.70pp (vs 8B 4.20pp) → MARGINAL for selection power, but QE correlations robust\\n- SciERC RE 4B: 47.6% zero-F1, RE LP_ρ_cond=0.1135 (p=0.074, n.s.) → confirms binary dominance at 4B\\n- **4B selection results** (correlation-selection gap at 4B):\\n  - CoNLL NER 4B (n=2756): SJ +0.07pp, FK +0.03pp, LP +0.09pp vs greedy 0.9213. All ≤0.1pp — MARGINAL (headroom 1.70pp)\\n  - SciERC NER 4B (n=529): SJ **-1.49pp**, FK -1.56pp, LP -2.23pp vs greedy 0.6622. Oracle 0.7925 (+13.02pp). **All signals WORSE than greedy despite 13pp oracle headroom — gap confirmed at 4B**\\n  - SciERC NER 4B gap demonstration: SJ-FK ρ gap = +0.096 (strong correlation advantage), SJ-FK selection F1 gap = +0.0007 (zero selection advantage) → correlation-selection gap quantified at 4B\\n- **n_samples confound control**: Qwen3-8B n=8 data exists as control. CoNLL SJ: n8 ρ=0.4389 → n16 ρ=0.4496 (Δ=0.011, CI crosses zero). SciERC SJ: n8→n16 Δ=0.066, CI=[0.004, 0.128] weak effect. n_samples not a confound for 4B-8B comparison.\\n- **Paper framing**: \\\"Scale ablation shows the gap is not an artifact of a particular model size; correlation strength is maintained at 4B, confirming that the gap originates from within-instance signal misalignment rather than model capacity.\\\"\\n\\n**exp-019 supervised verifier**: IN PROGRESS (D116 Dream feedback — all 3 tiers flagged as critical gap). Simplified to logistic regression on 5 signals (scikit-learn, CPU, ~30min). If supervised verifier partially closes oracle gap → proves gap IS closable, signals carry information but unsupervised heuristics are too weak. Grounds the \\\"what would be needed\\\" part of Contribution B.\\n\\n**exp-023 LoRA rank=8 ablation** [DONE]: rank=8 vs rank=32 on SciERC. Degeneracy: 7.2% vs 21% (3× reduction). Oracle headroom: 16.0pp vs 13.3pp. But ALL selection methods still fail to beat greedy (LP -0.5pp, SJ -0.9pp). QE correlation preserved (VC ρ=0.394, SJ ρ=0.365). **Key insight**: Reducing degeneracy is NECESSARY but NOT SUFFICIENT — signals themselves lack the discriminative power to exploit increased headroom. Strengthens multi-causal argument and points to trained verifiers as the path forward. Dream 三档公认最强实验。\\n\\n**exp-021 Few-NERD Full** [DONE — Scenario B: LP works, structural fails]:\\n- Dataset: Few-NERD INTRA coarse 8-type, 131k train, 32,565 test (gold-filtered)\\n- Degeneracy: 10.0% (vs SciERC 21%, CoNLL 58%) — lowest in our study\\n- LP selection: +1.36pp vs greedy (95% CI [+1.02, +1.34], p<0.05, bootstrap B=10000). **First dataset where LP significantly improves selection.**\\n- Structural signals: SJ -0.42pp, FK -0.55pp, EM -0.31pp, VC -0.18pp — ALL fail\\n- QE correlation (unified pipeline): LP ρ=0.22 (global), SJ ρ=0.38, FK ρ=0.35 — QE preserved; within-instance LP ρ≈0.5-0.7\\n- Oracle headroom: 13.8pp — ample room for selection\\n- **Key insight**: LP succeeds where LP-F1 alignment is strong; structural signals fail regardless. Within Few-NERD, per-entity-type analysis shows ρ=−0.881 between degeneracy and LP delta (within-dataset causal effect). But cross-dataset, the gradient CoNLL→SciERC→Few-NERD does NOT monotonically predict LP effectiveness (D154: ρ=0.052 at 23 points).\\n\\n**Bonus finding (parse rate)**: Free-form parse rate 99.95% (4406/4408) — fine-tuned 8B model generates valid JSON without grammar constraints. Challenges intro:19 claim about constrained decoding necessity for format compliance.\\n\\n**Implications**: Not just \\\"signals don't work\\\" but \\\"here's WHY they don't work (intrinsic score compression), here's proof it's universal (controlled ablation via elimination), and here's the forward direction (breaking LP compression requires fundamentally different approaches — trained verifiers, multi-round refinement — not simply relaxing decoding constraints).\\\"\\n\\n### Claim 2 (METHODOLOGY — QE Evaluation Framework)\\nSystematic evaluation of 5 confidence signals across 4 families for structured IE quality estimation. This is the methodological infrastructure, not a primary contribution.\\n\\n**Key results**:\\n- NER: EM AUROC 0.749, voting_conf ρ=0.379, SJ ρ=0.360 — all >> logprob (AUROC 0.578, ρ=0.205). 3-seed consistent: SJ > FK delta=0.093~0.122\\n- RE (3-seed confirmed): **LP > SJ > FK** (mean ρ: LP 0.302, SJ 0.265, FK 0.254). Signal ranking reverses vs NER — structural signals lose advantage when 43-45% of RE samples have F1=0 (binary signal dominance). LP's coarse but stable generation-confidence signal captures \\\"did the model get any relations right\\\" better than structural overlap measures.\\n- Difficulty-dependent signal ranking: WNUT-17 conditional collapse (ρ significant → n.s.) + logprob ρ=−0.410 (non-obvious: overconfidence on OOD)\\n- Temperature robustness: SJ Δρ=±0.01 vs FK Δρ=±0.06-0.09. T=0.7: LP full ρ improves 0.205→0.249, LP cond ρ 0.154→0.185; FK 0.267→0.289 despite more compression → concentrated signal hypothesis (non-tied instances at lower T carry more informative variance). **Paper citation rule**: robustness/temperature tables use full ρ; score compression analysis uses cond ρ. Always annotate which variant.\\n- **Task-dependent signal effectiveness** (new finding from RE 3-seed): The optimal signal family depends on task structure. NER (token-level, graded F1) → structural agreeme\\n\\n... [截断: 省略 28968 字符，传 max_chars=0 获取完整内容]\", \"total_chars\": 40968, \"truncated\": true}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

The Director's acknowledged my findings and included the ASB greedy_f1 bug fix in the action items. Now I'm mapping the paper's three core claims against the experimental coverage: the QE Framework with selection diagnostics, the evaluation framework spanning five signals across four families, and the prescriptive framework. The first claim has extensive support from scaling experiments across datasets, but I need to verify how thoroughly the second claim is covered.

Looking at what's actually done versus what's still pending, I see SCD completing today which is critical for the prescriptive contribution, several entity-level methods showing negative results, and cross-task experiments still in preparation. The 14B and 32B scaling runs are in progress with inference timelines around 30 hours and 4.5 hours respectively. With the deadline five days away, I'm weighing what's realistically achievable—the SCD results should wrap up within hours, but the larger scaling experiments will be tight, and cross-model consistency hasn't started yet.

The remaining experiments queue up across different resource types: some are CPU-only and queued, others like HumanEval and Frontier SC are API-based and should resolve in hours, while Spider SQL and ACE05 need GPU access. For the 05-25 deadline, writing has to start by the 24th, which means all critical results need to come in by the 23rd. The current timeline suggests the main experiments finish within two days, and the API-based cross-task work should be quick, so I don't see a major deadline risk emerging—the GPU budget looks sufficient and the team can run things in parallel.

Now I'm waiting for the SCD experiments to complete while I review the report.md file for any gaps or inconsistencies with what I've already observed across the code reviews, results, and GPU compliance checks.

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_013zq89KEBtKyNEhXTb5ndcx`</summary>

````text
1	# Experiment: exp_a3_ace05_re — Relation Extraction Self-Consistency Diagnostic
2	
3	## Objective
4	
5	Validate that the SC diagnostic cascade (degeneracy → agreement → LP-quality gap) generalizes from NER to Relation Extraction. RE is the closest structured prediction extension: same span-based output, but with entity pairs + relation types instead of single entity spans.
6	
7	## Data Source
8	
9	### Primary: SciERC (test split)
10	
11	- **Why SciERC, not ACE05**: ACE05 requires LDC license (restricted access, no public download). SciERC is publicly available, already integrated in the pipeline (`data_utils.load_scierc()`), and has both entity and relation annotations.
12	- **Dataset**: SciERC test set (~551 sentences from scientific abstracts)
13	- **Entity types** (6): Generic, Material, Method, Metric, OtherScientificTerm, Task
14	- **Relation types** (7): COMPARE, CONJUNCTION, EVALUATE-FOR, FEATURE-OF, HYPONYM-OF, PART-OF, USED-FOR
15	- **Format**: DyGIE++ JSON → converted to UIE format via `_parse_scierc_sentence()`
16	- **Download**: `bash scripts/data/get_scierc.sh` from DyGIE++ repo, or already present on server
17	
18	### Filtering
19	
20	Only instances with ≥1 gold relation are included in RE evaluation (skip relation-free sentences). Expected: ~200–300 instances after filtering.
21	
22	## Model
23	
24	- **Base**: Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct (same as NER experiments for controlled comparison)
25	- **Prompt**: Few-shot RE prompt with schema hint
26	- **Constrained decoding**: XGrammar with UIE_JSON_SCHEMA (already supports relations field)
27	
28	## Sampling Configuration
29	
30	| Parameter | Value |
31	|-----------|-------|
32	| N (samples) | 8 |
33	| Temperature | 1.0 |
34	| max_tokens | 1024 |
35	| use_grammar | True |
36	| collect_logprobs | True |
37	| subtask | "re" |
38	
39	Greedy baseline: T=0, N=1 (automatic via `run_sampling_pipeline`).
40	
41	## Prompt Template
42	
43	Uses `build_uie_prompt(text, subtask="re", schema_hint=SCIERC_SCHEMA_HINT)` which produces:
44	
45	```
46	Extract all relations (head entity, relation type, tail entity) from the following text.
47	Output a JSON object with the fields: entities, relations, events.
48	
49	Text: {text}
50	Schema: Entity types: Generic, Material, Method, Metric, OtherScientificTerm, Task
51	Relation types: COMPARE, CONJUNCTION, EVALUATE-FOR, FEATURE-OF, HYPONYM-OF, PART-OF, USED-FOR
52	
53	Output format: {"entities": [], "relations": [{"head": "...", "tail": "...", "type": "...", "head_start": &lt;int>, "head_end": &lt;int>, "tail_start": &lt;int>, "tail_end": &lt;int>}], "events": []}
54	```
55	
56	Alternative: `use_train_format=True` for the train-aligned template (extracts entities + relations jointly).
57	
58	## Output JSON Schema
59	
60	Same as `UIE_JSON_SCHEMA` in sampling.py. Relation fields:
61	
62	```json
63	{
64	  "head": "entity text",
65	  "tail": "entity text", 
66	  "type": "USED-FOR",
67	  "head_start": 10,
68	  "head_end": 25,
69	  "tail_start": 30,
70	  "tail_end": 45
71	}
72	```
73	
74	## Evaluation Metrics
75	
76	### Primary: Relation Strict Match F1
77	
78	`relation_strict_match()` in evaluation.py — (head_start, head_end, tail_start, tail_end, type) must all match exactly. Micro-averaged P/R/F1.
79	
80	### SC Diagnostic Metrics (per-instance)
81	
82	1. **Degeneracy**: All N samples produce identical relation sets → `is_degenerate_re()`
83	2. **Fleiss' Kappa (surface)**: `fleiss_kappa_surface(samples, subtask="re")` using (head, tail, type) surface keys
84	3. **Structural Soft Jaccard**: `structural_consistency_soft_jaccard(samples, subtask="re")` with `_re_soft_jaccard_pair()` — head/tail span overlap × type match via Hungarian matching
85	4. **Oracle Best-of-N**: `oracle_best_of_n(samples, gold, subtask="re")`
86	5. **LP-weighted construction**: Adapted from NER consensus_construct to aggregate relation triples
87	
88	### SC Failure Mode Diagnostics (F1–F4)
89	
90	| Mode | Definition (RE adaptation) |
91	|------|---------------------------|
92	| F1: Degeneracy | All 8 samples produce identical relation sets |
93	| F2: Low agreement | Fleiss' κ < 0.3 AND non-degenerate |
94	| F3: High agreement, low quality | κ ≥ 0.3 but greedy F1 < 0.3 |
95	| F4: LP-quality gap | construction_F1 − greedy_F1 < −0.05 (LP hurts) |
96	
97	## Pipeline Reuse
98	
99	| Component | Reuse | Notes |
100	|-----------|-------|-------|
101	| VLLMSampler | 100% | No changes needed |
102	| XGrammar schema | 100% | UIE_JSON_SCHEMA already has relations |
103	| build_uie_prompt | 100% | subtask="re" template exists |
104	| parse_extraction_output | 100% | _validate_relations() exists |
105	| realign_spans | 100% | Handles relation head/tail spans |
106	| run_sampling_pipeline | 100% | Pass subtask="re" |
107	| fleiss_kappa_surface | 100% | RE surface keys implemented |
108	| structural_consistency_soft_jaccard | 100% | _re_soft_jaccard_pair() exists |
109	| oracle_best_of_n | 100% | Uses per_instance_f1 with subtask="re" |
110	| relation_strict_match | 100% | Already in evaluation.py |
111	| consensus_construct | ~30% | NER-specific, needs RE adaptation |
112	| is_degenerate | ~30% | NER-specific, needs RE adaptation |
113	| bootstrap test | ~50% | Needs RE-specific key extraction |
114	
115	## GPU Requirements
116	
117	- **Model**: Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct → ~15 GB VRAM
118	- **Server**: autodl-5090 (single GPU sufficient)
119	- **Estimated time**: ~15 min for 300 instances × 8 samples with vLLM batched inference
120	- **Disk**: <100 MB for output JSONL
121	
122	## Dependencies
123	
124	```
125	vllm, transformers, xgrammar, torch
126	numpy, scipy, datasets (for SciERC loading)
127	```
128	
129	All already installed on autodl-5090 from NER experiments.
130	
````

The registry search for "exp_a3_ace05_re" returned empty. This might be because the registry uses a different lookup mechanism. Let me search directly in the registry file. But first, let me read the main code files.

The spec looks reasonable:
- Uses SciERC RE (instead of ACE05 due to LDC restrictions) — reasonable choice
- Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct (not fine-tuned) — this is a zero-shot/few-shot model
- N=8, T=1.0, same as NER experiments


- F1-F4 failure modes adapted for RE with about 85% pipeline reuse

But there's a critical inconsistency: the RE experiments are using the base Instruct model without fine-tuning, while the NER experiments used fine-tuned models. If the goal is controlled comparison, shouldn't RE also use a fine-tuned variant to make the comparison fair?

Actually, thinking about this more carefully — the purpose of exp_a3 is testing cross-task universality of the diagnostic cascade, not comparing absolute model performance. Using the same base model across both tasks is defensible because it isolates the framework's generalizability. Zero-shot RE will naturally have lower F1, which actually gives more diagnostic signal rather than less, and avoids confounding the framework test with model-specific tuning effects.

Now let me look at the code to understand the implementation details.

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01XzJXEHnBurVviyKYsGTJqR`</summary>

```text
1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""Relation Extraction Self-Consistency diagnostic experiment.
3	
4	Runs the full SC pipeline on SciERC RE data:
5	1. Load prepared RE instances (from prepare_re_data.py)
6	2. Sample N completions per instance via VLLMSampler + XGrammar
7	3. Compute RE F1 (greedy, majority vote, LP-weighted construction, oracle)
8	4. Compute SC diagnostic signals (degeneracy, Fleiss' κ, soft Jaccard)
9	5. Classify failure modes F1–F4
10	6. Run paired bootstrap test (construction vs greedy)
11	
12	Usage:
13	    # Full pipeline (sampling + evaluation)
14	    python run_re_sc.py \
15	        --model-path /root/autodl-tmp/models/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct \
16	        --data /root/autodl-tmp/struct_self_consist_ie/data/re_test_instances.jsonl \
17	        --output-dir /root/autodl-tmp/struct_self_consist_ie/output/re_sc
18	
19	    # Evaluation only (skip sampling, load existing samples)
20	    python run_re_sc.py \
21	        --samples /path/to/re_samples.jsonl \
22	        --output-dir /path/to/output \
23	        --eval-only
24	"""
25	
26	import argparse
27	import json
28	import os
29	import statistics
30	import sys
31	from collections import Counter, defaultdict
32	
33	import numpy as np
34	
35	sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "../.."))
36	
37	from sampling import (
38	    VLLMSampler,
39	    SCIERC_SCHEMA_HINT,
40	    run_sampling_pipeline,
41	    load_sampled_results,
42	    save_sampled_results,
43	)
44	from evaluation import (
45	    per_instance_f1,
46	    relation_strict_match,
47	    compute_re_f1,
48	    _prf,
49	)
50	from consistency import (
51	    fleiss_kappa_surface,
52	    structural_consistency_soft_jaccard,
53	    oracle_best_of_n,
54	    compute_all_consistency_scores,
55	)
56	from data_utils import load_uie_jsonl
57	
58	
59	SUBTASK = "re"
60	
61	
62	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
63	# RE-specific consensus construction
64	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
65	
66	def relation_key(r: dict) -> tuple:
67	    return (r["head_start"], r["head_end"], r["tail_start"], r["tail_end"], r["type"])
68	
69	
70	def consensus_construct_re(
71	    inst: dict,
72	    high_thresh: float = 0.5,
73	    medium_thresh: float = 0.25,
74	) -> list[dict]:
75	    """LP-weighted relation construction (adapted from NER consensus_construct)."""
76	    samples = inst["samples"]
77	    N = len(samples)
78	
79	    rel_counts: Counter = Counter()
80	    rel_sample_indices: dict[tuple, list[int]] = defaultdict(list)
81	    rel_repr: dict[tuple, dict] = {}
82	
83	    for i, s in enumerate(samples):
84	        seen: set[tuple] = set()
85	        for r in s.get("relations", []):
86	            key = relation_key(r)
87	            if key not in seen:
88	                rel_counts[key] += 1
89	                rel_sample_indices[key].append(i)
90	                seen.add(key)
91	                if key not in rel_repr:
92	                    rel_repr[key] = r
93	
94	    sample_lps = []
95	    for i, s in enumerate(samples):
96	        lp = s.get("mean_logprob")
97	        if lp is None and "logprobs" in inst and i < len(inst["logprobs"]):
98	            lp = inst["logprobs"][i]
99	        if lp is not None:
100	            sample_lps.append(lp)
101	    instance_median_lp = statistics.median(sample_lps) if sample_lps else 0.0
102	
103	    consensus = []
104	    for key, count in rel_counts.items():
105	        freq = count / N
106	        if freq > high_thresh:
107	            consensus.append(rel_repr[key])
108	        elif freq > medium_thresh:
109	            contributing_lps = []
110	            for i in rel_sample_indices[key]:
111	                lp = samples[i].get("mean_logprob")
112	                if lp is None and "logprobs" in inst and i < len(inst["logprobs"]):
113	                    lp = inst["logprobs"][i]
114	                if lp is not None:
115	                    contributing_lps.append(lp)
116	            if contributing_lps:
117	                mean_lp = statistics.mean(contributing_lps)
118	                if mean_lp > instance_median_lp:
119	                    consensus.append(rel_repr[key])
120	
121	    return consensus
122	
123	
124	def majority_vote_re(
125	    samples: list[dict],
126	    threshold: float = 0.5,
127	) -> list[dict]:
128	    """Majority vote for relation triples at a given frequency threshold."""
129	    N = len(samples)
130	    rel_counts: Counter = Counter()
131	    rel_repr: dict[tuple, dict] = {}
132	
133	    for s in samples:
134	        seen: set[tuple] = set()
135	        for r in s.get("relations", []):
136	            key = relation_key(r)
137	            if key not in seen:
138	                rel_counts[key] += 1
139	                seen.add(key)
140	                if key not in rel_repr:
141	                    rel_repr[key] = r
142	
143	    return [rel_repr[key] for key, count in rel_counts.items() if count / N >= threshold]
144	
145	
146	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
147	# Degeneracy detection (RE-specific)
148	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
149	
150	def is_degenerate_re(inst: dict) -> bool:
151	    """All samples produce identical relation sets."""
152	    key_sets: set[frozenset] = set()
153	    for s in inst["samples"]:
154	        ks = frozenset(relation_key(r) for r in s.get("relations", []))
155	        key_sets.add(ks)
156	    return len(key_sets) == 1
157	
158	
159	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
160	# Failure mode classification
161	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
162	
163	def classify_failure_mode(
164	    inst: dict,
165	    greedy_f1: float,
166	    construction_f1: float,
167	    kappa: float,
168	) -> str | None:
169	    """Classify instance into F1–F4 failure modes.
170	
171	    Returns mode string or None if no failure detected.
172	    """
173	    degenerate = is_degenerate_re(inst)
174	
175	    if degenerate:
176	        return "F1_degeneracy"
177	    if kappa < 0.3:
178	        return "F2_low_agreement"
179	    if kappa >= 0.3 and greedy_f1 < 0.3:
180	        return "F3_high_agreement_low_quality"
181	    if construction_f1 - greedy_f1 < -0.05:
182	        return "F4_lp_quality_gap"
183	    return None
184	
185	
186	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
187	# Main evaluation logic
188	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
189	
190	def evaluate_re_sc(
191	    sampled_instances: list[dict],
192	    output_dir: str,
193	    high_thresh: float = 0.5,
194	    medium_thresh: float = 0.25,
195	) -> dict:
196	    """Run full RE SC diagnostic evaluation."""
197	    N_samples = len(sampled_instances[0]["samples"]) if sampled_instances else 0
198	    theta = 2 / N_samples if N_samples > 0 else 0.25
199	
200	    if medium_thresh is None:
201	        medium_thresh = theta
202	
203	    results = {
204	        "n_instances": len(sampled_instances),
205	        "n_samples": N_samples,
206	        "high_thresh": high_thresh,
207	        "medium_thresh": medium_thresh,
208	    }
209	
210	    # --- Per-instance metrics ---
211	    greedy_f1s = []
212	    construction_f1s = []
213	    majority_f1s = []
214	    oracle_f1s = []
215	    kappas = []
216	    soft_jaccards = []
217	    degen_flags = []
218	    failure_modes: list[str | None] = []
219	
220	    for inst in sampled_instances:
221	        gold = inst["gold"]
222	        samples = inst["samples"]
223	
224	        greedy = inst.get("greedy", samples[0])
225	        g_f1 = per_instance_f1(greedy, gold, subtask=SUBTASK)
226	        greedy_f1s.append(g_f1)
227	
228	        construction_rels = consensus_construct_re(inst, high_thresh, medium_thresh)
229	        c_pred = {"relations": construction_rels}
230	        c_f1 = per_instance_f1(c_pred, gold, subtask=SUBTASK)
231	        construction_f1s.append(c_f1)
232	
233	        mv_rels = majority_vote_re(samples, threshold=0.5)
234	        mv_pred = {"relations": mv_rels}
235	        mv_f1 = per_instance_f1(mv_pred, gold, subtask=SUBTASK)
236	        majority_f1s.append(mv_f1)
237	
238	        _, o_f1 = oracle_best_of_n(samples, gold, subtask=SUBTASK)
239	        oracle_f1s.append(o_f1)
240	
241	        kappa = fleiss_kappa_surface(samples, subtask=SUBTASK)
242	        kappas.append(kappa)
243	
244	        sj = structural_consistency_soft_jaccard(samples, subtask=SUBTASK)
245	        soft_jaccards.append(sj)
246	
247	        degen = is_degenerate_re(inst)
248	        degen_flags.append(degen)
249	
250	        fm = classify_failure_mode(inst, g_f1, c_f1, kappa)
251	        failure_modes.append(fm)
252	
253	    # --- Aggregate metrics ---
254	    results["greedy_f1_mean"] = float(np.mean(greedy_f1s))
255	    results["construction_f1_mean"] = float(np.mean(construction_f1s))
256	    results["majority_vote_f1_mean"] = float(np.mean(majority_f1s))
257	    results["oracle_f1_mean"] = float(np.mean(oracle_f1s))
258	    results["fleiss_kappa_mean"] = float(np.mean(kappas))
259	    results["soft_jaccard_mean"] = float(np.mean(soft_jaccards))
260	
261	    n = len(sampled_instances)
262	    n_degen = sum(degen_flags)
263	    results["degeneracy_rate"] = n_degen / n if n > 0 else 0.0
264	    results["n_degenerate"] = n_degen
265	    results["n_nondegenerate"] = n - n_degen
266	
267	    # --- Micro-averaged F1 ---
268	    greedy_preds = [inst.get("greedy", inst["samples"][0]) for inst in sampled_instances]
269	    golds = [inst["gold"] for inst in sampled_instances]
270	    results["greedy_micro_f1"] = compute_re_f1(greedy_preds, golds)
271	
272	    construction_preds = []
273	    for inst in sampled_instances:
274	        construction_preds.append({"relations": consensus_construct_re(inst, high_thresh, medium_thresh)})
275	    results["construction_micro_f1"] = compute_re_f1(construction_preds, golds)
276	
277	    # --- Failure mode distribution ---
278	    fm_counts: Counter = Counter()
279	    for fm in failure_modes:
280	        fm_counts[fm or "none"] += 1
281	    results["failure_modes"] = dict(fm_counts)
282	    results["failure_mode_pcts"] = {k: v / n * 100 for k, v in fm_counts.items()} if n > 0 else {}
283	
284	    # --- Non-degenerate stratification ---
285	    if n - n_degen > 0:
286	        nd_mask = [not d for d in degen_flags]
287	        nd_greedy = [f for f, m in zip(greedy_f1s, nd_mask) if m]
288	        nd_construction = [f for f, m in zip(construction_f1s, nd_mask) if m]
289	        results["nondegen_greedy_f1_mean"] = float(np.mean(nd_greedy))
290	        results["nondegen_construction_f1_mean"] = float(np.mean(nd_construction))
291	        results["nondegen_diff"] = results["nondegen_construction_f1_mean"] - results["nondegen_greedy_f1_mean"]
292	
293	    # --- Paired bootstrap test ---
294	    results["bootstrap"] = run_bootstrap_re(greedy_f1s, construction_f1s, degen_flags)
295	
296	    # --- Per-instance data (for downstream analysis) ---
297	    per_instance = []
298	    for i, inst in enumerate(sampled_instances):
299	        per_instance.append({
300	            "id": inst["id"],
301	            "greedy_f1": greedy_f1s[i],
302	            "construction_f1": construction_f1s[i],
303	            "majority_vote_f1": majority_f1s[i],
304	            "oracle_f1": oracle_f1s[i],
305	            "fleiss_kappa": kappas[i],
306	            "soft_jaccard": soft_jaccards[i],
307	            "degenerate": degen_flags[i],
308	            "failure_mode": failure_modes[i],
309	            "n_gold_relations": len(inst["gold"].get("relations", [])),
310	        })
311	
312	    # --- Save outputs ---
313	    os.makedirs(output_dir, exist_ok=True)
314	
315	    with open(os.path.join(output_dir, "re_sc_results.json"), "w") as f:
316	        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)
317	
318	    with open(os.path.join(output_dir, "re_per_instance.json"), "w") as f:
319	        json.dump(per_instance, f, indent=2)
320	
321	    print_summary(results)
322	    return results
323	
324	
325	def run_bootstrap_re(
326	    greedy_f1s: list[float],
327	    construction_f1s: list[float],
328	    degen_flags: list[bool],
329	    B: int = 10000,
330	    seed: int = 42,
331	) -> dict:
332	    """Paired bootstrap test: greedy vs LP-weighted construction for RE."""
333	    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
334	    n = len(greedy_f1s)
335	
336	    greedy_arr = np.array(greedy_f1s)
337	    construction_arr = np.array(construction_f1s)
338	    diffs = construction_arr - greedy_arr
339	
340	    obs_diff = float(diffs.mean())
341	
342	    boot_diffs = np.zeros(B)
343	    for b in range(B):
344	        idx = rng.randint(0, n, n)
345	        boot_diffs[b] = diffs[idx].mean()
346	    boot_diffs.sort()
347	
348	    ci_lo = float(boot_diffs[int(0.025 * B)])
349	    ci_hi = float(boot_diffs[int(0.975 * B)])
350	    p_value = float(np.mean(boot_diffs <= 0))
351	
352	    std_diff = float(diffs.std(ddof=1))
353	    cohens_d = obs_diff / std_diff if std_diff > 0 else float("inf")
354	
355	    result = {
356	        "observed_diff": obs_diff,
357	        "ci_95": [ci_lo, ci_hi],
358	        "p_value": p_value,
359	        "cohens_d": cohens_d,
360	        "B": B,
361	        "n_positive": int(np.sum(diffs > 0)),
362	        "n_negative": int(np.sum(diffs < 0)),
363	        "n_tied": int(np.sum(diffs == 0)),
364	    }
365	
366	    degen_arr = np.array(degen_flags, dtype=bool)
367	    nd_mask = ~degen_arr
368	    if nd_mask.sum() > 0:
369	        nd_diffs = diffs[nd_mask]
370	        nd_n = nd_diffs.shape[0]
371	        nd_boot = np.zeros(B)
372	        for b in range(B):
373	            idx = rng.randint(0, nd_n, nd_n)
374	            nd_boot[b] = nd_diffs[idx].mean()
375	        nd_boot.sort()
376	        result["nondegen_observed_diff"] = float(nd_diffs.mean())
377	        result["nondegen_ci_95"] = [float(nd_boot[int(0.025 * B)]), float(nd_boot[int(0.975 * B)])]
378	        result["nondegen_p_value"] = float(np.mean(nd_boot <= 0))
379	
380	    return result
381	
382	
383	def print_summary(results: dict):
384	    """Print human-readable summary."""
385	    n = results["n_instances"]
386	    print(f"\n{'='*60}")
387	    print(f"  RE Self-Consistency Diagnostic — {n} instances")
388	    print(f"{'='*60}")
389	    print(f"Greedy F1 (mean):         {results['greedy_f1_mean']:.4f}")
390	    print(f"Construction F1 (mean):   {results['construction_f1_mean']:.4f}")
391	    print(f"Majority Vote F1 (mean):  {results['majority_vote_f1_mean']:.4f}")
392	    print(f"Oracle F1 (mean):         {results['oracle_f1_mean']:.4f}")
393	    print(f"Fleiss' κ (mean):         {results['fleiss_kappa_mean']:.4f}")
394	    print(f"Soft Jaccard (mean):      {results['soft_jaccard_mean']:.4f}")
395	    print(f"Degeneracy rate:          {results['degeneracy_rate']:.2%} ({results['n_degenerate']}/{n})")
396	
397	    micro = results.get("greedy_micro_f1", {})
398	    print(f"\nMicro-avg greedy:         P={micro.get('precision',0):.4f} R={micro.get('recall',0):.4f} F1={micro.get('f1',0):.4f}")
399	    micro_c = results.get("construction_micro_f1", {})
400	    print(f"Micro-avg construction:   P={micro_c.get('precision',0):.4f} R={micro_c.get('recall',0):.4f} F1={micro_c.get('f1',0):.4f}")
401	
402	    if "nondegen_diff" in results:
403	        print(f"\nNon-degen greedy F1:      {results['nondegen_greedy_f1_mean']:.4f}")
404	        print(f"Non-degen construction:   {results['nondegen_construction_f1_mean']:.4f}")
405	        print(f"Non-degen diff:           {results['nondegen_diff']:+.4f}")
406	
407	    print(f"\nFailure mode distribution:")
408	    for mode, pct in sorted(results.get("failure_mode_pcts", {}).items()):
409	        print(f"  {mode}: {pct:.1f}%")
410	
411	    boot = results.get("bootstrap", {})
412	    if boot:
413	        print(f"\nBootstrap (B={boot.get('B', 10000)}):")
414	        print(f"  Diff: {boot.get('observed_diff', 0):+.4f}")
415	        ci = boot.get("ci_95", [0, 0])
416	        print(f"  95% CI: [{ci[0]:+.4f}, {ci[1]:+.4f}]")
417	        print(f"  p-value: {boot.get('p_value', 1):.4f}")
418	
419	
420	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
421	# Main
422	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
423	
424	def main():
425	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="RE Self-Consistency Diagnostic")
426	    parser.add_argument("--model-path", default=None,
427	                        help="Path to Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct model")
428	    parser.add_argument("--data", default=None,
429	                        help="Path to prepared RE instances JSONL (from prepare_re_data.py)")
430	    parser.add_argument("--samples", default=None,
431	                        help="Path to existing sampled results JSONL (skip sampling)")
432	    parser.add_argument("--output-dir", required=True,
433	                        help="Output directory")
434	    parser.add_argument("--eval-only", action="store_true",
435	                        help="Skip sampling, load --samples directly")
436	    parser.add_argument("--n-samples", type=int, default=8)
437	    parser.add_argument("--temperature", type=float, default=1.0)
438	    parser.add_argument("--max-tokens", type=int, default=1024)
439	    parser.add_argument("--tensor-parallel", type=int, default=1)
440	    parser.add_argument("--use-train-format", action="store_true",
441	                        help="Use train-aligned prompt template (joint NER+RE)")
442	    parser.add_argument("--high-thresh", type=float, default=0.5)
443	    parser.add_argument("--medium-thresh", type=float, default=None,
444	                        help="LP threshold (default: 2/N)")
445	    args = parser.parse_args()
446	
447	    if args.eval_only:
448	        if not args.samples:
449	            parser.error("--samples required when --eval-only is set")
450	        sampled = load_sampled_results(args.samples)
451	    else:
452	        if not args.model_path or not args.data:
453	            parser.error("--model-path and --data required for sampling")
454	
455	        instances = load_uie_jsonl(args.data)
456	        print(f"Loaded {len(instances)} RE instances")
457	
458	        sampler = VLLMSampler(
459	            model_path=args.model_path,
460	            tensor_parallel_size=args.tensor_parallel,
461	            max_model_len=4096,
462	            gpu_memory_utilization=0.90,
463	        )
464	
465	        sampled = run_sampling_pipeline(
466	            sampler=sampler,
467	            instances=instances,
468	            n_samples=args.n_samples,
469	            temperature=args.temperature,
470	            max_tokens=args.max_tokens,
471	            subtask="re" if not args.use_train_format else "full",
472	            schema_hint=SCIERC_SCHEMA_HINT,
473	            use_grammar=True,
474	            use_chat_template=True,
475	            use_train_format=args.use_train_format,
476	            output_path=os.path.join(args.output_dir, "re_samples.jsonl"),
477	            realign=True,
478	            collect_logprobs=True,
479	        )
480	        print(f"Sampled {len(sampled)} instances × {args.n_samples} samples")
481	
482	    evaluate_re_sc(
483	        sampled,
484	        output_dir=args.output_dir,
485	        high_thresh=args.high_thresh,
486	        medium_thresh=args.medium_thresh,
487	    )
488	
489	
490	if __name__ == "__main__":
491	    main()
492	
```

rel_sample_indices: dict[tuple, list[int]] = defaultdict(list)\n    rel_repr: dict[tuple, dict] = {}\n\n    for i, s in enumerate(samples):\n        seen: set[tuple] = set()\n        for r in s.get(\"relations\", []):\n            key = relation_key(r)\n            if key not in seen:\n                rel_counts[key] += 1\n                rel_sample_indices[key].append(i)\n                seen.add(key)\n                if key not in rel_repr:\n                    rel_repr[key] = r\n\n    sample_lps = []\n    for i, s in enumerate(samples):\n        lp = s.get(\"mean_logprob\")\n statistics.median(sample_lps) if sample_lps else 0.0\n\n    consensus = []\n    for key, count in rel_counts.items():\n        freq = count / N\n        if freq > high_thresh:\n            consensus.append(rel_repr[key])\n        elif freq > medium_thresh:\n            contributing_lps = []\n            for i in rel_sample_indices[key]:\n                lp = samples[i].get(\"mean_logprob\")\n                if lp is None and \"logprobs\" in inst and i < len(inst[\"logprobs\"]):\n                    lp = inst[\"logprobs\"][i]\n                if lp is not None:\n                    contributing_lps.append(lp)\n            if contributing_lps:\n                mean_lp = statistics.mean(contributing_lps)\n                if mean_lp > instance_median_lp:\n                    consensus.append(rel_repr[key])\n\n    return consensus\n\n\ndef majority_vote_re(\n    samples: list[dict],\n    threshold: float = 0.5,\n) -> list[dict]:\n    \"\"\"Majority vote for relation triples at a given frequency threshold.\"\"\"\n    N = len(samples)\n    rel_counts: Counter = Counter()\n    rel_repr: dict[tuple, dict] = {}\n\n    for s in samples:\n        seen: set[tuple] = set()\n        for r in s.get(\"relations\", []):\n            key = relation_key(r)\n            if key not in seen:\n                rel_counts[key] += 1\n                seen.add(key)\n                if key not in rel_repr:\n                    rel_repr[key] = r\n\n    return [rel_repr[key] for key, count in rel_counts.items() if count / N >= threshold]\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Degeneracy detection (RE-specific)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef is_degenerate_re(inst: dict) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"All samples produce identical relation sets.\"\"\"\n    key_sets: set[frozenset] = set()\n    for s in inst[\"samples\"]:\n        ks = frozenset(relation_key(r) for r in s.get(\"relations\", []))\n        key_sets.add(ks)\n    return len(key_sets) == 1\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Failure mode classification\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef classify_failure_mode(\n    inst: dict,\n    greedy_f1: float,\n    construction_f1: float,\n    kappa: float,\n) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Classify instance into F1–F4 failure modes.\n\n    Returns mode string or None if no failure detected.\n    \"\"\"\n    degenerate = is_degenerate_re(inst)\n\n    if degenerate:\n        return \"F1_degeneracy\"\n    if kappa < 0.3:\n        return \"F2_low_agreement\"\n    if kappa >= 0.3 and greedy_f1 < 0.3:\n        return \"F3_high_agreement_low_quality\"\n    if construction_f1 - greedy_f1 < -0.05:\n        return \"F4_lp_quality_gap\"\n    return None\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Main evaluation logic\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef evaluate_re_sc(\n    sampled_instances: list[dict],\n    output_dir: str,\n    high_thresh: float = 0.5,\n    medium_thresh: float = 0.25,\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Run full RE SC diagnostic evaluation.\"\"\"\n    N_samples = len(sampled_instances[0][\"samples\"]) if sampled_instances else 0\n    theta = 2 / N_samples if N_samples > 0 else 0.25\n\n    if medium_thresh is None:\n        medium_thresh = theta\n\n    results = {\n        \"n_instances\": len(sampled_instances),\n        \"n_samples\": N_samples,\n        \"high_thresh\": high_thresh,\n        \"medium_thresh\": medium_thresh,\n    }\n\n    # --- Per-instance metrics ---\n    greedy_f1s = []\n    construction_f1s = []\n    majority_f1s = []\n    oracle_f1s = []\n    kappas = []\n    soft_jaccards = []\n    degen_flags = []\n    failure_modes: list[str | None] = []\n\n    for inst in sampled_instances:\n        gold = inst[\"gold\"]\n        samples = inst[\"samples\"]\n\n        greedy = inst.get(\"greedy\", samples[0])\n        g_f1 = per_instance_f1(greedy, gold, subtask=SUBTASK)\n        greedy_f1s.append(g_f1)\n\n        construction_rels = consensus_construct_re(inst, high_thresh, medium_thresh)\n        c_pred = {\"relations\": construction_rels}\n        c_f1 = per_instance_f1(c_pred, gold, subtask=SUBTASK)\n        construction_f1s.append(c_f1)\n\n        mv_rels = majority_vote_re(samples, threshold=0.5)\n        mv_pred = {\"relations\": mv_rels}\n        mv_f1 = per_instance_f1(mv_pred, gold, subtask=SUBTASK)\n        majority_f1s.append(mv_f1)\n\n        _, o_f1 = oracle_best_of_n(samples, gold, subtask=SUBTASK)\n        oracle_f1s.append(o_f1)\n\n        kappa = fleiss_kappa_surface(samples, subtask=SUBTASK)\n        kappas.append(kappa)\n\n        sj = structural_consistency_soft_jaccard(samples, subtask=SUBTASK)\n        soft_jaccards.append(sj)\n\n        degen = is_degenerate_re(inst)\n        degen_flags.append(degen)\n\n        fm = classify_failure_mode(inst, g_f1, c_f1, kappa)\n        failure_modes.append(fm)\n\n    # --- Aggregate metrics ---\n    results[\"greedy_f1_mean\"] = float(np.mean(greedy_f1s))\n    results[\"construction_f1_mean\"] = float(np.mean(construction_f1s))\n    results[\"majority_vote_f1_mean\"] = float(np.mean(majority_f1s))\n    results[\"oracle_f1_mean\"] = float(np.mean(oracle_f1s))\n    results[\"fleiss_kappa_mean\"] = float(np.mean(kappas))\n    results[\"soft_jaccard_mean\"] = float(np.mean(soft_jaccards))\n\n    n = len(sampled_instances)\n    n_degen = sum(degen_flags)\n    results[\"degeneracy_rate\"] = n_degen / n if n > 0 else 0.0\n    results[\"n_degenerate\"] = n_degen\n    results[\"n_nondegenerate\"] = n - n_degen\n\n    # --- Micro-averaged F1 ---\n    greedy_preds = [inst.get(\"greedy\", inst[\"samples\"][0]) for inst in sampled_instances]\n    golds = [inst[\"gold\"] for inst in sampled_instances]\n    results[\"greedy_micro_f1\"] = compute_re_f1(greedy_preds, golds)\n\n    construction_preds = []\n    for inst in sampled_instances:\n        construction_preds.append({\"relations\": consensus_construct_re(inst, high_thresh, medium_thresh)})\n    results[\"construction_micro_f1\"] = compute_re_f1(construction_preds, golds)\n\n    # --- Failure mode distribution ---\n    fm_counts: Counter = Counter()\n    for fm in failure_modes:\n        fm_counts[fm or \"none\"] += 1\n    results[\"failure_modes\"] = dict(fm_counts)\n    results[\"failure_mode_pcts\"] = {k: v / n * 100 for k, v in fm_counts.items()} if n > 0 else {}\n\n    # --- Non-degenerate stratification ---\n    if n - n_degen > 0:\n        nd_mask = [not d for d in degen_flags]\n        nd_greedy = [f for f, m in zip(greedy_f1s, nd_mask) if m]\n        nd_construction = [f for f, m in zip(construction_f1s, nd_mask) if m]\n        results[\"nondegen_greedy_f1_mean\"] = float(np.mean(nd_greedy))\n        results[\"nondegen_construction_f1_mean\"] = float(np.mean(nd_construction))\n        results[\"nondegen_diff\"] = results[\"nondegen_construction_f1_mean\"] - results[\"nondegen_greedy_f1_mean\"]\n\n    # --- Paired bootstrap test ---\n    results[\"bootstrap\"] = run_bootstrap_re(greedy_f1s, construction_f1s, degen_flags)\n\n    # --- Per-instance data (for downstream analysis) ---\n    per_instance = []\n    for i, inst in enumerate(sampled_instances):\n        per_instance.append({\n            \"id\": inst[\"id\"],\n            \"greedy_f1\": greedy_f1s[i],\n            \"construction_f1\": construction_f1s[i],\n            \"majority_vote_f1\": majority_f1s[i],\n            \"oracle_f1\": oracle_f1s[i],\n            \"fleiss_kappa\": kappas[i],\n            \"soft_jaccard\": soft_jaccards[i],\n            \"degenerate\": degen_flags[i],\n            \"failure_mode\": failure_modes[i],\n            \"n_gold_relations\": len(inst[\"gold\"].get(\"relations\", [])),\n        })\n\n    # --- Save outputs ---\n    os.makedirs(output_dir, exist_ok=True)\n\n    with open(os.path.join(output_dir, \"re_sc_results.json\"), \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)\n\n    with open(os.path.join(output_dir, \"re_per_instance.json\"), \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(per_instance, f, indent=2)\n\n    print_summary(results)\n    return results\n\n\ndef run_bootstrap_re(\n    greedy_f1s: list[float],\n    construction_f1s: list[float],\n    degen_flags: list[bool],\n    B: int = 10000,\n    seed: int = 42,\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Paired bootstrap test: greedy vs LP-weighted construction for RE.\"\"\"\n    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)\n    n = len(greedy_f1s)\n\n    greedy_arr = np.array(greedy_f1s)\n    construction_arr = np.array(construction_f1s)\n    diffs = construction_arr - greedy_arr\n\n    obs_diff = float(diffs.mean())\n\n    boot_diffs = np.zeros(B)\n    for b in range(B):\n        idx = rng.randint(0, n, n)\n        boot_diffs[b] = diffs[idx].mean()\n    boot_diffs.sort()\n\n    ci_lo = float(boot_diffs[int(0.025 * B)])\n    ci_hi = float(boot_diffs[int(0.975 * B)])\n    p_value = float(np.mean(boot_diffs <= 0))\n\n    std_diff = float(diffs.std(ddof=1))\n    cohens_d = obs_diff / std_diff if std_diff > 0 else float(\"inf\")\n\n    result = {\n        \"observed_diff\": obs_diff,\n        \"ci_95\": [ci_lo, ci_hi],\n        \"p_value\": p_value,\n        \"cohens_d\": cohens_d,\n        \"B\": B,\n        \"n_positive\": int(np.sum(diffs > 0)),\n        \"n_negative\": int(np.sum(diffs < 0)),\n        \"n_tied\": int(np.sum(diffs == 0)),\n    }\n\n    degen_arr = np.array(degen_flags, dtype=bool)\n    nd_mask = ~degen_arr\n    if nd_mask.sum() > 0:\n        nd_diffs = diffs[nd_mask]\n        nd_n = nd_diffs.shape[0]\n        nd_boot = np.zeros(B)\n        for b in range(B):\n            idx = rng.randint(0, nd_n, nd_n)\n            nd_boot[b] = nd_diffs[idx].mean()\n        nd_boot.sort()\n        result[\"nondegen_observed_diff\"] = float(nd_diffs.mean())\n        result[\"nondegen_ci_95\"] = [float(nd_boot[int(0.025 * B)]), float(nd_boot[int(0.975 * B)])]\n        result[\"nondegen_p_value\"] = float(np.mean(nd_boot <= 0))\n\n    return result\n\n\ndef print_summary(results: dict):\n    \"\"\"Print human-readable summary.\"\"\"\n    n = results[\"n_instances\"]\n    print(f\"\\n{'='*60}\")\n    print(f\"  RE Self-Consistency {results['construction_f1_mean']:.4f}\")\n    print(f\"Majority Vote F1 (mean):  {results['majority_vote_f1_mean']:.4f}\")\n    print(f\"Oracle F1 (mean):         {results['oracle_f1_mean']:.4f}\")\n    print(f\"Fleiss' κ (mean):         {results['fleiss_kappa_mean']:.4f}\")\n    print(f\"Soft Jaccard (mean):      {results['soft_jaccard_mean']:.4f}\")\n    print(f\"Degeneracy rate:          {results['degeneracy_rate']:.2%} ({results['n_degenerate']}/{n})\")\n\n    micro = results.get(\"greedy_micro_f1\", {})\n    print(f\"\\nMicro-avg greedy:         P={micro.get('precision',0):.4f} R={micro.get('recall',0):.4f} F1={micro.get('f1',0):.4f}\")\n    micro_c = results.get(\"construction_micro_f1\", {})\n    print(f\"Micro-avg construction:   P={micro_c.get('precision',0):.4f} R={micro_c.get('recall',0):.4f} F1={micro_c.get('f1',0):.4f}\")\n\n    if \"nondegen_diff\" in results:\n        print(f\"\\nNon-degen greedy F1:      {results['nondegen_greedy_f1_mean']:.4f}\")\n        print(f\"Non-degen construction:   {results['nondegen_construction_f1_mean']:.4f}\")\n        print(f\"Non-degen diff:           {results['nondegen_diff']:+.4f}\")\n\n    print(f\"\\nFailure mode distribution:\")\n    for mode, pct in sorted(results.get(\"failure_mode_pcts\", {}).items()):\n        print(f\"  {mode}: {pct:.1f}%\")\n\n    boot = results.get(\"bootstrap\", {})\n    if boot:\n        print(f\"\\nBootstrap (B={boot.get('B', 10000)}):\")\n        print(f\"  Diff: {boot.get('observed_diff', 0):+.4f}\")\n        ci = boot.get(\"ci_95\", [0, 0])\n        print(f\"  95% CI: [{ci[0]:+.4f}, {ci[1]:+.4f}]\")\n        print(f\"  p-value: {boot.get('p_value', 1):.4f}\")\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Main\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef main():\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\"RE Self-Consistency Diagnostic\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--model-path\", default=None,\n                        help=\"Path to Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct model\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--data\", default=None,\n <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The code looks well-structured. Let me analyze it for issues:

1. **Micro vs Macro F1 consistency**: The code computes BOTH macro (mean per-instance) and micro (aggregate TP/FP/FN) F1. This is good — D204 requires micro F1 as the standard. Line 254 computes `greedy_f1_mean` (macro), lines 268-275 compute `greedy_micro_f1` and `construction_micro_f1` (micro). Both are saved. This is consistent with D204.

2. **Bootstrap test**: Uses paired bootstrap (B=10000, seed=42). 

The bootstrap procedure tests whether construction significantly outperforms greedy by computing the p-value as the proportion of bootstrap differences that are non-positive, which directly tests if construction is better. The stratification ensures non-degenerate sampling across the dataset.

3. **Consensus construction**: The LP-weighted construction adapts the NER consensus to RE, using relation tuples as keys and applying a high threshold (>0.5) for automatic inclusion and a medium threshold (>0.25) for LP-conditional inclusion.

4. **Potential issue: `per_instance_f1` for RE**: The per-instance F1 calculation needs to correctly handle RE evaluation, likely through an internal call to `relation_strict_match`. If this function is well-tested from prior work, it should be reliable.

5. **Potential issue: `compute_re_f1` vs per_instance_f1**: There's a consistency concern between the micro-average F1 computation and the per-instance F1 calculation — both need to define relation matches the same way.

6. **Medium_thresh default**: The default medium threshold is set to 2/N_samples (0.25 for N=8), and I need to check how this behaves when `medium_thresh` is not explicitly provided.

7. **Greedy handling**: The code falls back to the first sample if no explicit greedy sample exists, but the sampling pipeline should automatically generate a greedy baseline, so this fallback shouldn't actually occur in normal operation.

8. **F4 failure mode**: The F4 metric flags cases where construction F1 drops more than 5 percentage points below greedy F1, indicating the LP construction is harming performance.

9. **Seed reproducibility**: There's no explicit seed parameter for sampling in the main function, though the bootstrap uses seed=42. The sampling seed depends on the VLLMSampler implementation, which could be an issue for reproducibility.

10. **Model selection**: The spec correctly uses Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct without fine-tuning, aligning with the goal of testing framework universality on new tasks.

The code is generally well-structured, though adding an explicit `--seed` parameter would improve reproducibility, and there are a few other minor concerns worth addressing.

One thing I'm noticing is that the macro F1 computation is still being calculated even though the paper should only report micro F1 based on earlier feedback. The code computes both metrics, which is fine for internal validation, but the final results need to stick with micro F1 only.
```

1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""Prepare SciERC data for Relation Extraction SC experiments.

4	Loads SciERC test split, filters to instances with ≥1 gold relation,

14	import argparse

16	import os

18	import sys

26	SCHEMA_HINT = SCIERC_SCHEMA_HINT
27	
28	RE_FEWSHOT_INSTRUCTION = (
29	    "Extract all relations (head entity, relation type, tail entity) from the given text. "
30	    "Output a JSON object with fields: entities, relations, events.\n\n"
31	    f"{SCHEMA_HINT}\n\n"
32	    "Output format: "
33	    '{"entities": [], '
34	    '"relations": [{"head": "...", "tail": "...", "type": "...", '
35	    '"head_start": &lt;int>, "head_end": &lt;int>, "tail_start": &lt;int>, "tail_end": &lt;int>}], '
36	    '"events": []}\n'
37	)
38	
39	
40	def instance_to_demo(inst: Instance) -> str:
41	    """Format a gold instance as a few-shot demonstration."""
42	    text = inst["text"]
43	    gold_rels = inst.get("relations", [])
44	    gold_json = json.dumps(
45	        {"entities": [], "relations": gold_rels, "events": []},
46	        ensure_ascii=False,
47	    )
48	    return f"Text: {text}\nOutput: {gold_json}"
49	
50	
51	def build_fewshot_prompt(
52	    inst: Instance,
53	    demos: list[Instance],
54	) -> str:
55	    """Build a few-shot RE prompt for a test instance."""
56	    parts = [RE_FEWSHOT_INSTRUCTION]
57	    for i, demo in enumerate(demos):
58	        parts.append(f"### Example {i + 1}\n{instance_to_demo(demo)}")
59	    parts.append(f"### Now extract:\nText: {inst['text']}\nOutput:")
60	    return "\n\n".join(parts)
61	
62	
63	def build_zeroshot_prompt(inst: Instance) -> str:
64	    """Build a zero-shot RE prompt for a test instance."""
65	    return (
66	        f"{RE_FEWSHOT_INSTRUCTION}\n"
67	        f"Text: {inst['text']}\nOutput:"
68	    )
69	
70	
71	def select_demos(
72	    train_instances: list[Instance],
73	    k: int,
74	    seed: int = 42,
75	) -> list[Instance]:
76	    """Select k diverse few-shot demonstrations from training set.
77	
78	    Picks instances that have relations AND cover diverse relation types.
79	    """
80	    rel_instances = [inst for inst in train_instances if inst.get("relations")]
81	    if not rel_instances:
82	        return []
83	
84	    rng = random.Random(seed)
85	
86	    seen_types: set[str] = set()
87	    selected: list[Instance] = []
88	
89	    rng.shuffle(rel_instances)
90	    for inst in rel_instances:
91	        rel_types = {r["type"] for r in inst["relations"]}
92	        new_types = rel_types - seen_types
93	        if new_types:
94	            selected.append(inst)
95	            seen_types |= rel_types
96	            if len(selected) >= k:
97	                break
98	
99	    while len(selected) < k and len(selected) < len(rel_instances):
100	        candidate = rng.choice(rel_instances)
101	        if candidate not in selected:
102	            selected.append(candidate)
103	
104	    return selected[:k]
105	
106	
107	def main():
108	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Prepare SciERC RE data for SC experiments")
109	    parser.add_argument("--scierc-dir", required=True, help="Path to SciERC data directory")
110	    parser.add_argument("--output", required=True, help="Output JSONL path for test instances")
111	    parser.add_argument("--demo-output", default=None, help="Output path for few-shot demos JSON")
112	    parser.add_argument("--few-shot-k", type=int, default=3, help="Number of few-shot examples")
113	    parser.add_argument("--prompt-mode", choices=["zeroshot", "fewshot", "train_aligned"],
114	                        default="fewshot", help="Prompt construction mode")
115	    parser.add_argument("--min-relations", type=int, default=1,
116	                        help="Minimum gold relations to include instance")
117	    parser.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=42)
118	    parser.add_argument("--stats", action="store_true", help="Print dataset statistics")
119	    args = parser.parse_args()
120	
121	    splits = load_scierc(args.scierc_dir)
122	    train_all = splits["train"]
123	    test_all = splits["test"]
124	
125	    test_re = [inst for inst in test_all if len(inst.get("relations", [])) >= args.min_relations]
126	
127	    if args.stats:
128	        for split_name, insts in [("train", train_all), ("test", test_all), ("test_re_filtered", test_re)]:
129	            n_rels = sum(len(inst.get("relations", [])) for inst in insts)
130	            n_with_rel = sum(1 for inst in insts if inst.get("relations"))
131	            rel_types = set()
132	            for inst in insts:
133	                for r in inst.get("relations", []):
134	                    rel_types.add(r["type"])
135	            print(f"{split_name}: {len(insts)} instances, {n_with_rel} with relations, "
136	                  f"{n_rels} total relations, types: {sorted(rel_types)}")
137	
138	    demos = select_demos(train_all, args.few_shot_k, seed=args.seed)
139	
140	    output_instances = []
141	    for inst in test_re:
142	        if args.prompt_mode == "fewshot":
143	            prompt = build_fewshot_prompt(inst, demos)
144	        elif args.prompt_mode == "zeroshot":
145	            prompt = build_zeroshot_prompt(inst)
146	        else:
147	            prompt = None
148	
149	        record = {
150	            "id": inst["id"],
151	            "text": inst["text"],
152	            "entities": inst.get("entities", []),
153	            "relations": inst.get("relations", []),
154	            "events": [],
155	        }
156	        if prompt is not None:
157	            record["prompt"] = prompt
158	
159	        output_instances.append(record)
160	
161	    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(args.output) or ".", exist_ok=True)
162	    with open(args.output, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
163	        for inst in output_instances:
164	            f.write(json.dumps(inst, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
165	
166	    print(f"Wrote {len(output_instances)} RE instances to {args.output}")
167	    print(f"  Relation types in test set: {sorted({r['type'] for inst in output_instances for r in inst['relations']})}")
168	    print(f"  Total gold relations: {sum(len(inst['relations']) for inst in output_instances)}")
169	
170	    if args.demo_output and demos:
171	        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(args.demo_output) or ".", exist_ok=True)
172	        with open(args.demo_output, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
173	            json.dump([dict(d) for d in demos], f, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)
174	        print(f"Wrote {len(demos)} few-shot demos to {args.demo_output}")

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The data preparation script looks good. Let me summarize my findings:

**Code Quality**:
- Well-structured, clean code
- Proper separation between data preparation and evaluation
- Bootstrap test implementation is correct
- Both micro and macro F1 are computed
- Few-shot demo selection is diverse (covers different relation types)
- Proper filtering (only instances with ≥1 gold relation)

**Issues to flag**:

1. **No --seed in run_re_sc.py**: The sampling doesn't have an explicit seed parameter. `prepare_re_data.py` has `--seed 42`, but `run_re_sc.py` doesn't pass a seed to the VLLMSampler or the sampling pipeline. This affects reproducibility.


2. **Model choice**: The experiment uses Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct without fine-tuning, which differs from the NER experiments that used fine-tuned models. While the registry spec calls for consistency, using the base model provides a defensible zero-shot baseline—especially since earlier fine-tuned RE experiments (ft_001/ft_002) achieved ρ=0.24, so this will offer a useful comparison point.
3. **Data already exists**: SciERC RE data was already used in early pilots (D003-D010), and the pipeline already supports RE evaluation, making this experiment somewhat redundant with existing work.

The key difference is using Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct instead of the earlier fine-tuned Qwen3-8B, which could yield additional insights. I need to verify that SCIERC_SCHEMA_HINT is correctly defined in sampling.py, though since it's reusing existing infrastructure it should be fine. There's also a potential issue with the demo formatting—the gold_json is created with empty entities even when the instance has them, which is intentional for a relations-only prompt, but it means the model is asked to output entities as empty despite the joint extraction format expecting them.

The registry search came up empty for exp_a3_ace05_re, likely a tool issue, so I should verify via grep. Before posting the review, I should also check whether this experiment overlaps with the existing SciERC RE data mentioned in paper_framing.md, which showed different model performance (LP > SJ > FK). Since this new experiment uses zero-shot Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct rather than fine-tuned models, it's a different setup that should provide distinct data points.

There's a naming mismatch I need to flag: the registry hypothesis mentions ACE05 Relation Extraction for cross-task universality testing, but the actual experiment was switched to SciERC RE while keeping the exp_a3_ace05_re name. Also, the spec lists autodl-5090 as the server, but the ROADMAP references specific GPU assignments (cuda:1 or cuda:3 on autodl-5090-c), so I should note this potential discrepancy before finalizing the review.
```

### Assistant

_source line 1307 · 2026-05-20T18:43:31.044Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The kill script looks reasonable:
1. Polls `/tmp/14b_fewnerd.log` every 15 seconds
2. When "=== FewNERD N=8 finished" is detected, kills the process tree
3. First kills children of SHELL_PID 200995, then the shell itself
4. Then pkill any remaining vllm/python processes matching the pattern

**Concern**: `pkill -f "VLLM::EngineCore"` will kill ALL VLLM EngineCore processes, not just the 14B one. There's also the T_low inference running on GPU 2, which uses VLLM::EngineCore (PID 221771). 

The pattern match would catch both the 14B inference on GPU 0 and the T_low inference on GPU 2, so the T_low process would get killed as collateral damage when the 14B finishes. By the time N=8 completes, this becomes a real problem.

Actually, looking more carefully at the kill script structure: it first targets the orchestrating script's process tree directly with `pkill -P` and `kill`, so the `pkill -f "VLLM::EngineCore"` fallback is less likely to trigger in normal execution. But it's still a design flaw — if T_low takes longer than expected or another VLLM process starts on the same server, the broad pattern match would terminate it unintentionally.

Since T_low should finish hours before the kill triggers, I'll mark this as an info-level note rather than a warning and move on to posting the review.
```

</details>

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am acting as an independent Reviewer (独立科学审查员) for the `struct_self_consist_ie` project targeting EMNLP 2026 Main Conference (ARR May cycle, deadline 2026-05-25). My role is to independently verify experiment conclusions, check GPU resource boundaries (L1 red line #4), assess spec completeness, monitor training progress, and report findings to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. All output must be in Chinese. I receive worker completion notifications and proactively check running experiments. This session continued from a previous conversation that was compacted.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Structured Self-Consistency (SC)**: Entity-level majority vote from N stochastic samples for IE/NER
   - **Self-Consistency Distillation (SCD)**: Fine-tune model on SC pseudo-labels to improve greedy performance; 3 variants running: standard (seed 42), fallback (seed 43, originally curriculum_q deferred per D189), threshold (θ=3/N ablation)
   - **Adaptive Threshold (E_θ)**: Learning per-instance optimal θ — negative result (MLP -0.34pp vs fixed θ=0.5, oracle gap only 0.13pp)
   - **Frontier SC**: Testing SC with GPT-5.5 + Claude Opus 4.6 on FewNERD (coarse 8-type NER)
   - **Macro vs Micro F1**: Critical cross-experiment inconsistency resolved by D204 (standardize to micro-F1)
   - **Cross-task universality**: Extending SC diagnostics to RE (SciERC), code generation (HumanEval), SQL (Spider)
   - **Kill-after-N=8**: Monitoring script to terminate 14B inference after N=8 (skipping N=16 to save ~14h)
   - **GPU boundary enforcement**: L1 red line requiring processes to only use GPUs within project's whitelist (all 12 GPUs across 3 servers whitelisted for this project)
   - **Servers**: autodl-5090 (4× RTX 5090), autodl-5090-b (4× RTX 5090), autodl-5090-c (4× RTX 5090)
   - **Paper target**: "Strong Predictors, Weak Selectors: Addressing the Correlation-Selection Gap in Self-Consistency for Named Entity Recognition" — EMNLP 2026 Main, deadline 2026-05-25

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **Project config** (`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_1/config/projects/struct_self_consist_ie.yaml`):
     - GPU constraints: all 4 GPUs (cuda:0-3) on all 3 servers whitelisted
     - Project path: `/root/autodl-tmp/struct_self_consist_ie`
     - Used to verify no GPU boundary violations

   - **`artifacts/exp_e_theta_adaptive/data/v2_mlp_results.json`** (602 lines):
     - Full MLP adaptive theta results: 18 configs (2 models × 3 datasets × 3 seeds)
     - Key numbers: mean_f1_fixed_05=0.7940, mean_f1_adaptive_mlp=0.7906 (-0.34pp), mean_f1_oracle=0.7953 (+0.13pp)
     - Win/Tie/Loss: 5/0/13 — MLP loses on 13/18 configs
     - pred_theta_raw often negative (e.g., -0.82 for CoNLL), clamped to 0.3
     - Qwen_FewNERD_s42 has n_instances=4351 (vs 32565 for other FewNERD configs) — data inconsistency

   - **`artifacts/experiments/ace05_re/spec.md`** (130 lines):
     - SciERC RE experiment spec (ACE05 replaced due to LDC restrictions)
     - Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, N=8, T=1.0, few-shot prompt
     - F1-F4 failure modes adapted for RE
     - Pipeline reuse ~85%

   - **`artifacts/experiments/ace05_re/run_re_sc.py`** (492 lines):
     - `relation_key(r)` → (head_start, head_end, tail_start, tail_end, type) five-tuple
     - `consensus_construct_re(inst, high_thresh=0.5, medium_thresh=0.25)` — LP-weighted relation construction
     - `majority_vote_re(samples, threshold=0.5)` — frequency-based MV for relations
     - `is_degenerate_re(inst)` — all samples produce identical relation sets
     - `classify_failure_mode(inst, greedy_f1, construction_f1, kappa)` → F1-F4 modes
     - `evaluate_re_sc(sampled_instances, output_dir)` — full diagnostic evaluation with micro+macro F1
     - `run_bootstrap_re(greedy_f1s, construction_f1s, degen_flags, B=10000, seed=42)` — paired bootstrap with non-degenerate stratification
     - Missing `--seed` parameter for sampling reproducibility (only bootstrap has seed=42)

   - **`artifacts/experiments/ace05_re/prepare_re_data.py`** (179 lines):
     - `select_demos(train_instances, k, seed=42)` — diverse few-shot demo selection covering different relation types
     - Filters test instances to those with ≥1 gold relation
     - Supports zeroshot/fewshot/train_aligned prompt modes

   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/kill_after_n8.sh`** (on autodl-5090-c):
     - Monitors `/tmp/14b_fewnerd.log` every 15 seconds
     - Kills process tree when "=== FewNERD N=8 finished" detected
     - Two-layer kill: `pkill -P $SHELL_PID` + `kill $SHELL_PID`, then fallback `pkill -f "VLLM::EngineCore"`
     - Risk: broad pkill could kill T_low inference (but T_low should finish hours before trigger)

   - **ROADMAP.md**: Updated with Track A (writing v3) + Track B (method breakthrough), D204 decision on micro-F1 standardization

   - **decisions.yaml** (D200-D204): Recent decisions including E_θ V1 negative (D200), entity-level methods all negative (D201-D202), scope expansion to 4.5 (D203), micro-F1 standardization (D204)

   - **report.md**: Handover document listing 6 GPU experiments running, 1 CPU experiment, 2 pending, 4 code-ready

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **Registry search returning empty for some exp_ids**: When searching by `exp_id` filter, some experiments (exp_asb_adaptive_budget, exp_e_theta_adaptive, exp_a3_ace05_re) returned 0 results despite existing in registry.yaml. Worked around by using Grep on the registry.yaml file directly.

   - **compute_coverage_matrix tool error**: `'<' not supported between instances of 'str' and 'int'` — tool bug, could not use. Relied on manual ROADMAP/registry analysis instead.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **GPU boundary compliance**: Verified all 12 GPUs across 3 servers. All processes within project whitelist. No violations.
   - **exp_e_theta_adaptive stale status**: Discovered experiment stuck in RUNNING for 11h, no local process running, only 1/5 variants produced results (v2_mlp). Recommended marking as DONE (negative).
   - **E_θ adaptive negative result validated**: MLP with 7 features on only 18 configs (leave-one-config-out CV with 17 training points) cannot learn the mapping. Oracle gap only 0.13pp — θ=0.5 is a strong default with limited room for improvement.
   - **D204 confirmation**: Verified that Director acknowledged macro/micro F1 inconsistency and ASB bug findings from previous session.
   - **14B kill script collateral risk identified**: `pkill -f "VLLM::EngineCore"` too broad, but T_low expected to finish before trigger — risk acceptable.
